### 6.20 Stand-ins of the planet mass law (GEN.711)

The planet mass law (`physics/planet_mass.py`, `docs/design/composition/planet-mass-law.md`) gives a planet its mass from the disc's solids at its formation distance, with the core-accretion rule that turns a heavy enough core into a gas giant. The numbers below are tuned by hand against the occurrence of planets (Aux 16's check, `reports/planet-class-distribution-check-2026-10-25.md`); the rates they are tuned to are recalled, not read. The law is wired into generation by the class cut-over (GEN.646); until then no generated value depends on these rows.

<!-- planet-mass-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| PM1 | P | T3 | L3 | tuning.PLANET_DISC_SOLIDS_ENHANCEMENT 5 (the solids of the disc against the minimum-mass nebula) | 3 to 10, with PM2 | hosts of 0.1 to 3 solar masses | test_planet_mass::test_a_sun_like_star_has_about_a_tenth_of_a_giant_between_a_third_and_ten_au; a measured inner-planet occurrence outside 0.5 to 0.9 per Sun-like star inside 0.45 AU | a disc-evolution model of the solids (pebble flux) from GEN.509 | RECALLED (Chiang and Laughlin 2013: the extrasolar nebula needs several times the minimum-mass solar nebula; Petigura et al. 2018 and Zhu et al. 2018: 0.5 to 0.9 planets of 1 to 4 Earth radii per Sun-like star); hand-tuned against a sweep of 400 systems |
| PM2 | P | T3 | L3 | tuning.PLANET_INNER_DISC_STEEPENING 0.75 (the solids rise faster than the nebula's -1.5 power inside 1 AU) | 0.5 to 1.0 | inside 1 AU | test_planet_mass::test_the_inner_disc_is_steeper_than_the_nebula_inside_one_au; an M dwarf occurrence of 1 to 20 Earth-mass planets inside 0.45 AU outside 1 to 2.5 | a pebble-drift model of the inner disc | RECALLED (Chiang and Laughlin 2013: minimum-mass extrasolar nebula about the -1.6 to -2 power; Dressing and Charbonneau 2015: about 2.5 planets of 0.5 to 4 Earth radii per M dwarf within 50 days); hand-tuned |
| PM3 | P | T7 | L0 | tuning.PLANET_ASSEMBLY_SCATTER_DEX 0.8 (log-normal spread of a planet's mass about the isolation mass times the assembly factor) | 0.4 to 1.0 | all hosts | the spread of the mass-radius sample (test_mass_radius sweep) outside 0.4 to 1.0 dex | N-body assembly runs (Chambers 2001, Kokubo and Ida) | design (no published width read; the sweep of reports/planet-mass-law-sweep-2026-10-24.md) |
| PM4 | T | T4 | L0 | tuning.GIANT_IMPACT_SURVIVAL_FRACTION 0.4 (the share of oligarchs that survive as planets, so a planet is 2.5 isolation masses) | 0.3 to 0.5 | all hosts | the planet count per star (estimate_num_objects) against the count of Kepler multis | a published N-body survival fraction | RECALLED (Chambers 2001); mass conservation |
| PM5 | T | T3 | L0 | tuning.GIANT_CORE_CRITICAL_MASS_EARTH 10 (the core that can run away into a gas giant) | 5 to 20 | beyond the snow line | a measured critical core outside 5 to 20 Earth masses | a core-accretion model with the opacity of the envelope | RECALLED (Pollack et al. 1996; Rafikov 2006: 5 to 20 Earth masses) |
| PM6 | P | T3 | L3 | tuning.GIANT_GAS_SURVIVAL_SHARE 0.12 (the share of runaway-capable cores that find gas left, at solar metallicity) | 0.05 to 0.2 | beyond the snow line, within the cut distance | test_planet_mass::test_a_sun_like_star_has_about_a_tenth_of_a_giant_between_a_third_and_ten_au; a giant occurrence outside 0.10 to 0.15 per Sun-like star in 0.3 to 10 AU | a disc-lifetime model (GEN.510) | RECALLED (Fulton et al. 2021: 0.10 to 0.15 giants per Sun-like star in 0.3 to 10 AU; Johnson et al. 2010 and Bonfils et al. 2013 for M dwarfs); hand-tuned |

### 6.21 Stand-ins of the life timeline and the biogenic oxygen (GEN.750, GEN.855)

Rows BO1 to BO9 for the oxygen oxygenic life puts in the air (`physics/biogenic_oxygen.py`). The rows of the hard-step timeline of life (`physics/life_timeline.py`, `tuning.LIFE_*`) and of the gates of the light-harvesting chemistries are LF49 to LF56 in section 6.18, where the life constants are. Design note: `docs/design/habitability/life-timeline-and-oxygen.md`. Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725). Tiers and tweaks are the lane's reading (Core 1), for the owner to confirm. The Earth figures of BO1 to BO3 are the test point and the scale of the oxygen budget, not a preset: a world with another light, sea, heat, redox and gravity gets another oxygen.

### 6.22 Stand-ins of the class cut-over (GEN.646)

The class cut-over (GEN.646) makes a body from its mass, where it formed and its star, and derives the class letter from what it comes out as. The numbers below are the choices that cut-over adds; the mass law's own are 6.16. They are tuned by hand against Aux 16's class distribution check (`reports/planet-class-distribution-check-2026-10-25.md`) and the sweep of `reports/class-cutover-sweep-2026-10-28.md`; the rates they are tuned to are recalled, not read.

### 6.23 Stand-ins of the tidal rheology (GEN.954)

The tidal Love number and quality factor from the mantle's temperature, melt and the tide's frequency (`physics/tidal_rheology.py`, a pure module that nothing calls until GEN.955 wires it into `tidal_heating`, `interior_heat` and `orbit_elements`). The form is Andrade (Efroimsky 2012, Castillo-Rogez et al. 2011) on the Maxwell rheology of Henning, O'Connell and Sasselov 2009, with the body's own drawn mantle viscosity. The rows of 6.13 (TH1 to TH10) stay the registry of the built constant-Q rheology until GEN.955 retires them: it removes TH3, TH4 and TH5 and the pressure and melt stand-ins of TH6, TH7 and TH9 move here.

<!-- tidal-rheology-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TR1 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_rheology.MELT_VISCOSITY_FACTOR 26 (a partly molten mantle is exp(-26 phi) times as viscous as its solid) | 20 to 31 | a rocky mantle between the solidus and the critical melt fraction | test_tidal_rheology::test_viscosity_is_arrhenius_then_weakened_by_melt_then_a_liquid_at_full_melt | a melt-fraction viscosity law that depends on the grain size and the melt's shape | Hirth and Kohlstedt 2003 (diffusion creep, 26) and Mei et al. 2002 (26 to 31), recalled, not opened |
| TR2 | P | T3 | L1 | tidal_rheology.CRITICAL_MELT_FRACTION 0.4 (the melt share at which the crystals stop touching and the mush becomes a suspension) | 0.2 to 0.5 | a rocky mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_viscosity_is_arrhenius_then_weakened_by_melt_then_a_liquid_at_full_melt | a rheological transition that depends on the crystal size | the range in Arzi 1978 (0.2 to 0.4) and the 0.4 of Henning et al. 2009, recalled; the top of the range |
| TR3 | T | T6 | L3 | tidal_rheology.rigidity_pa and MELT_RIGIDITY_FLOOR 1e-3 (rigidity mu0 (1 - phi / phi_c)^2 down to a floor of a thousandth of the solid's) | floor 1e-4 to 1e-2 | a partly molten mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_rigidity_falls_with_melt_to_a_floor | a shear modulus from the melt's geometry (Hashin-Shtrikman bounds) | physics fixes the form (a suspension has no static rigidity), the size is argued; the floor is the lowest value that keeps the Io state's k2 under the fluid 3/2 |
| TR4 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_rheology.ANDRADE_EXPONENT 0.3 (the transient creep of grain boundaries) | 0.2 to 0.4 | a rocky mantle at tidal frequencies | test_tidal_rheology::test_the_moon_comes_out_with_the_lunar_laser_ranging_k2_and_q | the exponent from the grain size and the temperature | laboratory olivine 0.2 to 0.4; 0.3 in Jackson and Faul 2010 and Castillo-Rogez et al. 2011, recalled |
| TR5 | P | T6 | L0 | tidal_rheology.ANDRADE_TIMESCALE_RATIO 1 (the Andrade timescale over the Maxwell time) | 0.01 to 100 | a rocky mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_the_moon_comes_out_with_the_lunar_laser_ranging_k2_and_q | the ratio measured for a mantle rock | 1 in Efroimsky 2012; Renaud and Henning 2018 explore 0.01 to 100; the Moon's Q of 38 to 60 is reproduced at 1 without tuning |
| TR6 | T | T6 | L0 | tidal_rheology.love_number (a homogeneous incompressible sphere with the complex rigidity: one temperature and one melt share stand for the whole mantle; the core, the cold lid and Io's thin warm layer are not separate) | none | any body that is mostly one material | test_tidal_rheology::test_the_solid_earth_has_the_semidiurnal_tide_of_a_q_of_a_few_hundred | a layered Love number (propagator-matrix) | the form of Love 1911 with the correspondence principle; the solid Earth's k2 comes out 0.5 against the real 0.30 |
| TR7 | T | T6 | L0 | tidal_rheology.viscosity_pa_s above CRITICAL_MELT_FRACTION (a straight line in log10 viscosity to a liquid's 1 Pa s at full melt) | none | a mush above the critical melt fraction | test_tidal_rheology::test_a_magma_ocean_tends_to_the_fluid_love_number_and_no_dissipation | a suspension law (Roscoe-Einstein) | the form is chosen to be continuous at phi_c; the end points are the solid's value there and a silicate liquid |
| TR8 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_rheology.LIQUID_VISCOSITY_PA_S 1 (a silicate liquid) | 0.1 to 10 | a fully molten mantle | test_tidal_rheology::test_viscosity_is_arrhenius_then_weakened_by_melt_then_a_liquid_at_full_melt | a liquid viscosity from the melt's composition | komatiite to basalt melt, 0.1 to 10 Pa s, recalled |
| TR9 | P | T2 | L0 | tidal_rheology.ICE (rigidity 3.3e9 Pa), ICE_ACTIVATION_J_MOL 4.9e4, ICE_MELTING_VISCOSITY_PA_S 1e14 (ice I) | rigidity 3.3 to 3.5 GPa; activation 40 to 60 kJ/mol; viscosity 1e13 to 1e15 | an ice body or shell | test_tidal_rheology::test_cold_ice_gives_europa_the_k2_over_q_that_tidal_heating_uses_and_a_warm_shell_far_more | the shell treatment of GEN.960 (grain size, a shell on an ocean) | Gammon et al. 1983 (rigidity), Goldsby and Kohlstedt 2001 and McCarthy and Castillo-Rogez 2013 (creep), Ojakangas and Stevenson 1989 (viscosity), all second-hand |
| TR10 | P | T6 | L3 | tidal_rheology.COLD_Q_CEILING 1000 (the largest Q the Andrade form gives: a cold mantle and a magma ocean both keep the loss of rock's own friction and of a solid lid and core) | 300 to 5000 | every rocky body | test_tidal_rheology::test_a_very_cold_mantle_keeps_the_loss_of_cold_rock | a frictional and extrinsic loss law with temperature | Q of 500 to a few thousand in the Earth's lithosphere and the Moon's cold interior (Dziewonski and Anderson 1981, Nakamura 1982, recalled); without it 25 percent of generated planets get a Q above 6e4 and close-in magma-ocean planets never lock |
<!-- tidal-rheology-standins:end -->

### 6.24 Stand-ins of the spin torques (GEN.956)

The sum of the torques on a planet's spin: its star's tide, each moon's tide and the thermal tide of its air (`physics/spin_torques.py`, a pure module until GEN.957 wires it into `planets.generate_spin`). The gravitational torque keeps the built constant-Q form (SP7 of 6.14) or takes the frequency-dependent `k2/Q` of 6.23; the resonance windows are SP8 and SP9. The thermal torque is the Debye form of Leconte, Wu, Menou and Murray 2015 (Science 347:632, arXiv:1502.01952, read in full), whose amplitude is tabulated for four atmospheres only.

<!-- spin-torques-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TQ1 | P | T3 | L0 | spin_torques.GRAVITATIONAL_TORQUE_FACTOR 3 (the built despinning's constant; Murray and Dermott and Leconte et al. write 3/2 for the same constant-Q result; the thermal torque is scaled with it so their ratio stays the paper's) | 1.5 or 3 | every tide | test_spin_torques::test_the_moon_slows_the_earth_by_the_measured_two_milliseconds_a_century | one definition of Q for the whole chain (GEN.957 decides with the rheology) | the 3 is Gladman et al. 1996 as SP7 has it; at 3/2 the lunar torque gives the Earth's measured 2.0 ms per century |

### 6.26 Stand-ins of the birth spin from the last impacts (GEN.978)

The birth spin of a rocky planet from the mass ratio of its last impacts (`physics/impact_spin.py`, a pure module until GEN.979 wires it into `spin.accretion_spin`). The formula is Kokubo and Genda 2010 equation 4 (read in full text); the distribution of the mass ratio by planet mass is not published in a form we hold, so it is a stand-in, built to cover the widest range of the two sources in question: Kokubo and Genda's 2 to 3 hours and Miguel and Brunini 2010's 10 to 10,000 hours. SP1 to SP4 of 6.14 stay the registry of the built birth spin until GEN.979 retires them.

<!-- impact-spin-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| GS1 | T | T1 | L0 | impact_spin.g_function and spin_constant (omega_rms / omega_cr = (5 / sqrt 2) <sin^2 theta>^(1/2) nu_rms g(gamma), g = gamma (1 - gamma) [gamma^(1/3) + (1 - gamma)^(1/3)]^(1/2)) | none | the spin left by the last one or few collisions of a rocky planet | test_impact_spin::test_g_is_symmetric_and_has_the_values_of_the_paper | the paper's N-body statistics | Kokubo and Genda 2010 (ApJ 714:L21, arXiv:1003.4384) equation 4, read in full text |
| GS2 | P | T5 | L0 | impact_spin.MEAN_SIN2_THETA 0.5 (impact parameter uniform in area) | none | collisions in a disc | test_impact_spin::test_the_equation_reproduces_the_paper_s_own_rms_at_its_mass_ratio | the paper's measured angle distribution | geometry |
| GS3 | P | T6 | L2 | impact_spin.IMPACT_SPEED_RATIO 1.1 (the rms of impact speed over escape speed) | 1.0 to 1.3 | the last impacts of protoplanets | test_impact_spin::test_the_equation_reproduces_the_paper_s_own_rms_at_its_mass_ratio | the paper's mean of nu^2 | physics fixes the floor at 1 (a fall from rest at infinity); 1.1 gives the paper's rms 0.71 breakup at gamma 0.28 |
| GS4 | P | T6 | L3 | impact_spin.ISOLATION_MASS_EARTH 0.1 and ISOLATION_STEEPNESS 2 (the chance of a giant impact is 1 / (1 + (M_iso / M)^2)) | mass 0.05 to 0.2; steepness 1 to 4 | rocky planets of 0.01 to 10 Earth masses | test_impact_spin::test_mars_is_reachable_and_an_earth_mass_planet_seldom_turns_that_slowly | the distribution of the last impactor by planet mass from Kokubo and Ida 2007 or Agnor et al. 1999 | the isolation mass of oligarchic growth is 0.1 to 0.2 Earth masses at 1 AU (Kokubo and Ida 2000, recalled); the steepness is chosen so that Mars is as likely to have had a giant impact as not |
| GS5 | P | T6 | L3 | impact_spin.GIANT_GAMMA_RANGE (0.02, 0.5) (log-uniform mass ratio of the last giant impact) | lower end 0.01 to 0.1 | planets made by giant impacts | test_impact_spin::test_the_median_free_day_is_in_the_range_of_vesta_ceres_and_the_earth_moon_system | the N-body distribution of the last impactor | the last impactors run from a few percent of the planet to its own size (Agnor et al. 1999, Kokubo and Ida 2007, recalled); the lower end is chosen so the median day of 0.1 to 5 Earth masses is 7 hours |
| GS6 | P | T6 | L3 | impact_spin.PLANETESIMAL_GAMMA_RANGE (0.001, 0.03) (log-uniform effective mass ratio of an embryo built by planetesimals) | upper end 0.01 to 0.05 | embryos below the isolation mass | test_impact_spin::test_mars_is_reachable_and_an_earth_mass_planet_seldom_turns_that_slowly | a planetesimal-accretion spin law | the upper end is Mars (0.07 of breakup needs gamma 0.022); the lower end gives the slow spins of Miguel and Brunini 2010 (ordered accretion about 150 h at 1 AU) |
| GS7 | T | T6 | L0 | the widest range: spins from 1.3 hours (the breakup cap) to some thousand hours | none | a rocky planet before tides | test_impact_spin::test_the_range_is_the_widest_of_the_two_sources_in_question | a measured rotation of a rocky exoplanet or a sample of terrestrial planets (none exists) | Boss's rule for two sources in question: the widest range; Kokubo and Genda 2 to 3 hours, Miguel and Brunini 2010 (arXiv:1004.1406, read in full) 10 to 10,000 hours |

GEN.967 replaces the design defaults of `magnetism` with it.

### 6.31 Stand-ins of the accreting compact binaries (GEN.272, GEN.724)

The periods, donors and shares that `generation/multistar.py` draws for the X-ray binaries, microquasars and spider pulsars (`tuning.py`, the block that opens with the GEN.272 comment; design note `docs/design/systems/multi-star-systems.md` 13.4). Registered by GEN.724: `src/tests/test_accreting_standins.py` fails when a constant of that block has no row here. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1 and in the list of every stand-in. The registry row `millisecond-pulsar-companion` (`generation/phenomena/system_types.py`) carried 5 percent neutron-star companions for recycled pulsars; the code keeps 0.2 percent (Boss, 4:01 PM EDT 2026-10-24, card option 1) because 5 percent would be about ten times the double neutron stars the gravitational-wave rate allows, and the row now says so (AC10).

### 6.33 Stand-ins of the stellar spectra (GEN.970)

The spectra library (`datasets/stellar_spectra.py`, `physics/stellar_library.py`) reads two model-atmosphere grids (SL1, 6.6) and adds the chromosphere and corona from the eleven MUSCLES stars. Checked against the Sun's measured ultraviolet shares and against the near-ultraviolet of the eleven stars (`test_stellar_library_real.py`). The MUSCLES host (archive.stsci.edu) fails its TLS chain from the cloud machines, so the star-by-star fluxes are the paper's tables (France et al. 2016, Tables 1 and 3), not the HLSP files.

### 6.35 Stand-ins of the condensates by temperature (GEN.950)

`physics/condensate_shares.py` holds Lodders 2003 Tables 10 and 11 (the temperatures at which the ices condense and the mass of each condensate for the Solar System composition at 10^-4 bar) and gives the water share of the solids by disk temperature and by distance. The values are the paper's own; the open question is whether planetesimals follow nebular equilibrium (ice over rock about 2) or comets (about 1, Marboeuf et al. 2014, read second-hand in Emsenhuber et al. 2021), which the wiring step settles with the water-share switch of GEN.468.

<!-- condensate-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

### 6.36 Stand-ins of the planet architecture (GEN.728, GEN.729, GEN.730, GEN.828, GEN.829, GEN.920)

The planet architecture (`physics/planet_architecture.py`, `docs/design/composition/planet-architecture.md`) replaces the uniform planet count, the uniform spacing ratio and the constant 0.4 survival with a Kepler-like inner system drawn at a sourced share, a chain of oligarchs thinned by dynamical instability (Pu and Wu 2015), an outer chain at the isolation mass, a gas outcome and the migration of giants. The observed rates it is checked against are the sourced bands of `physics/data/planet_occurrence.json`, each READ or 2ND-HAND with its tier. Its own numbers are tuned together (the `ARCH_*` constants carry a copy of the mass law's disc and gas numbers, so the live law does not move until the generator is wired to the architecture). Tiers and tweaks are the lane's reading (Planets 1), for the owner to confirm.

<!-- planet-architecture-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |

| PA1 | P | T6 | L3 | tuning.ARCH_DISC_SOLIDS_ENHANCEMENT 0.4647, ARCH_OUTER_GROWTH_AU 8 and ARCH_OUTER_GROWTH_EXPONENT 2.726 (the solids of the disc against the minimum-mass nebula; where the outer disc's growth falls behind and how fast) | enhancement 0.3 to 1.5; growth distance 3 to 10; exponent 1.5 to 4 | beyond the snow line; hosts of 0.1 to 1.6 solar masses | test_planet_architecture::test_cold_giants_of_a_sun_like_star_are_as_common_as_the_survey_finds_them; a giant occurrence at 2 to 8 AU outside 0.12 to 0.16 per Sun-like star | a disc-evolution and pebble-flux model of the outer solids (GEN.509) | tuned to Fulton et al. 2021 (arXiv:2105.11584, READ) and the Suzuki et al. 2016 microlensing rate (recalled); the mass law's own PM1 and PM6 stay until the wiring |

### 6.37 Stand-ins of the brown dwarf ceiling (GEN.494)

`physics/substellar_ceiling.py` reads the Baraffe et al. 2015 (BHAC15) isochrones for the temperature of the hydrogen-burning limit mass at each age: the hottest a brown dwarf can be, about 2950 K at 10 Myr and falling to 2047 K at 1 Gyr and 1626 K at 10 Gyr. It gives the young late-M-like row (g1) of the atmosphere recipes its hot edge from a table instead of the 2800 K clip of the rogue bodies. The temperature map of Kirkpatrick et al. 2021 (Table 13) already gives the other edges (GEN.517, GEN.760).

<!-- substellar-ceiling-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SB1 | P | T1 | L0 | substellar_ceiling.LIMIT_MASS_SOLAR 0.07 (the hydrogen-burning limit of the solar-metallicity models; the table also holds 0.072) | none | solar metallicity | a model whose limit lies more than 0.005 solar masses from 0.07 (the limit rises at low [Fe/H], GEN.936) | the limit as a function of [Fe/H] from the Baraffe table (GEN.936) | test_substellar_ceiling::test_the_limit_mass_is_hottest_at_ten_million_years_as_the_paper_says |
| SB2 | P | T1 | L0 | substellar_ceiling.HOTTEST_K 2950 (the highest temperature the limit mass reaches; the table has 2946 K at 10 Myr) | none | any age from 0.5 Myr to 10 Gyr | an isochrone set whose limit mass peaks more than 100 K from 2950 K | a later set of models | test_substellar_ceiling::test_the_limit_mass_is_hottest_at_ten_million_years_as_the_paper_says |
<!-- substellar-ceiling-standins:end -->

### 6.38 Stand-ins of the aluminium-26 and iron-60 at birth (GEN.476)

`physics/birth_radioisotopes.py` builds the ratio of aluminium-26 and iron-60 to their stable partners at a star's birth from the Galaxy's gamma-ray masses of the two (the yield per mass of stars formed), the depletion time of the star's region and the granularity of the sources, replacing the log-uniform 0.1 to 10 times the Solar System's own 5.23e-5 of `radiogenic`. The Solar System ratio (and the iron-60 ratio of 1.0e-8) are test points only.

### 6.39 Stand-ins of the X-ray saturation time by mass (GEN.493)

`physics/saturation_time.py` gives the age at which a star falls out of X-ray saturation by mass (0.1 to 1.2 solar masses) and initial rotation percentile (the 5th, 50th and 95th of the 150 Myr distribution), read off Fig. 10 of Johnstone, Bartel and Guedel 2021. At one solar mass it matches Tu et al. 2015 (5.2, 22.8 and 297 Myr against 5.7, 23 and 226). It replaces the one set of solar-mass times that row p6 used for every mass.

<!-- saturation-time-standins:begin -->

### 6.42 Stand-ins of the brackets the research items GEN.480 and GEN.489 to GEN.493 leave (Sources 2)

The atmosphere recipe rows of GEN.480 (occurrence of water-rich and H/He worlds), GEN.489 (white dwarf and pulsar planets), GEN.490 (trace gases), GEN.491 (clouds and hazes), GEN.492 (radioisotopes) and GEN.493 (host-star rotation) were `DESIGN` rows with a bracket drawn by the seeded draw. Where research was read the bracket is narrowed or the row is sourced (rows g4 and p6 are now `DERIVED`; `substellar_ceiling`, `birth_radioisotopes` and `saturation_time` hold the numbers); what no source reaches is registered here, a constant of `scripts/atmosphere_recipes_data.py`.

### 6.43 Stand-ins of the mass left in a belt (GEN.477)

`physics/belt_mass.py` gives a planetesimal belt its mass from the disc's solids ground down by collisions (Wyatt et al. 2007, Eqs. 14 and 15), in place of the uniform draw of the density word and the zero mass of NAV.62. The Solar System's belt (0.6 thousandths of an Earth mass inferred) is a test point: the model leaves 0.5 to 2.6 thousandths for Ceres-size largest bodies.

<!-- belt-mass-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|

| BM2 | P | T9 | L4 | belt_mass.DENSE_FACTOR 3 (a belt with three times the reference mass per area is dense, a third sparse) | 2 to 10 | all belts | the dense and sparse shares of the generated belts outside what the words are meant to cover | an observed spread of belt masses at one age | test_belt_mass::test_the_density_word_follows_the_mass_per_area_against_its_reference |

### 6.44 Stand-ins of the chondrite-group map (GEN.475)

`physics/meteorite_groups.py` holds the chondrite groups as points of a four-axis composition space (olivine fayalite, metal, refractory inclusions and matrix, Weisberg et al. 2006 Table 1) and labels a body with its nearest group, so the groups become outputs of a derived bulk and no longer inputs to a body's mix.

### 6.45 Stand-ins of the rock types from bulk oxides (GEN.474)

`physics/rock_types.py` works out the ground type of a body from the oxides of its rock: the CIPW norm, the melt of a mantle by stages, and the silica bands of the total alkali-silica diagram. The Earth's oxide analyses (bulk silicate Earth, the three crust layers) and a lunar highland anorthosite are test points, never inputs.

### 6.46 Stand-ins of the comet ice budget (GEN.595)

`physics/comet_ice_budget.py` gives the CO2/H2O and CO/H2O of a comet's ice from the zone of the disc it formed in (the snow lines and the carbon and oxygen budget of Oberg, Murray-Clay and Bergin 2011), in place of the Solar System's measured population as the within-system shape. 2I/Borisov's CO/H2O (1.30 to 1.55) is a test of the CO zone; the Solar System's median comet and 3I/ATLAS's CO2/H2O of 7.6 are not reproduced and stay stand-ins in `comet_population`.

### 6.53 Stand-ins of the debris disks (GEN.787)

The cold debris belt of a main-sequence star (`physics/debris_disk.py`, `tuning.py` block that opens with the GEN.787 comment): the share of stars of each kind that hold one, the temperature of the dust, and the ring it makes. Source: Sibthorpe et al. 2018 (MNRAS 475, 3046; arXiv:1803.00072), the Herschel DEBRIS survey, read 2026-10-30 through a fetched copy that held its first part verbatim (the abstract, sections 2.5 and 3, Tables 1 and 2 as counted). The mass of a disk is not drawn: only the light of its dust is measured.

| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DD1 | B | T1 | L0 | tuning.DEBRIS_DISK_INCIDENCE (0.022, 0.130, 0.143, 0.239, 0.24) with DEBRIS_DISK_INCIDENCE_MASS_LIMITS_SOL (0.45, 0.8, 1.04, 1.4): the share of stars of each mass that hold a cold disk | M 0.5 to 6 percent, K 9 to 17, G 10 to 19, F 19 to 29, A 20 to 33 (the published errors; Su et al. 2006 give 33 percent for A) | detected discs, so the count of a survey with a median sensitivity of 2e-5 in fractional luminosity | a completeness-corrected incidence (27.7 percent of FGK stars) | the survey's volume-limited incidence by type | READ (Sibthorpe 2018 section 3 and Table 2) |
| DD2 | B | T3 | L0 | tuning.DEBRIS_DISK_INCIDENCE[-1] 0.24: a star above 1.4 solar masses takes the A figure | B stars unmeasured here | stars up to the Herschel sample's hottest | a B-star survey | a measured B-star incidence | Matthews et al. 2014, no clear dependence of the dust on type from late B to M (read in the summary part of the fetched copy) |
| DD3 | P | T6 | L2 | tuning.DEBRIS_DISK_TEMPERATURE_MEDIAN_K 58 and DEBRIS_DISK_TEMPERATURE_LOG_SIGMA 0.45: the dust temperature of a disk, log-normal | median 45 to 70 K, sigma 0.3 to 0.6 | stars of one solar luminosity | the 47 blackbody radii of Table 1 | the temperatures read from the table itself | median 23 AU and 7 to 40 AU for most, from the abstract and the table as counted |
| DD4 | P | T6 | L1 | tuning.DEBRIS_RADIUS_OVER_BLACKBODY 1.74: the belt lies this far beyond its blackbody radius | 1.5 to 2.5 (the paper: a blackbody radius "can underestimate the true disc radius by up to a factor of 2.5") | realistic dust | resolved belt radii | resolved radii from the imaged discs | "scales to about 40 AU for realistic dust properties" over a median of 23 AU |

### 6.56 Stand-ins of the registered rows that rest on a Solar System body (GEN.1002, GEN.1005)

A row of a registered possibility table whose weights, fractions or width come from the Moon, Mars, the Earth or one comet is a stand-in for what other worlds hold (Boss: no fixed content from the Milky Way or the Solar System; flagged stand-ins only). `tests/test_calibration_fence.py` scans every registered row for a body's name and lists the rows in `tests/data/solar_system_rows.json`; `tests/test_solar_system_standin_rows.py` requires each listed row to have a row here, and the registered row to carry the state `stand-in` and the replacement item named in the last but one column. The `constant` column starts with the row as `table:row`.

### 6.48 Stand-ins of the star counts' composition (GEN.661)

The number of stars a population puts in a mass band or above a luminosity (`generation/star_population.py`) depends on metallicity: a metal-poor star of one mass is brighter and hotter, so more of a metal-poor population is bright. The counts are worked out once for the whole population at the composition the population holds on average (`reference_fe_h`), not star by star. Each star's own birth [Fe/H] is drawn separately and does follow its component.

<!-- star-count-standins:begin -->
| fill_id | kind | tier | tweak | constant | knobs | validity | falsifier | replaced_by | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| SCC1 | D | T5 | L0 | star_population.reference_fe_h: a population's counts are worked out at its mean [Fe/H] at the Sun's distance (the disc has no radial gradient here, a component has no value of its own in the counts) | none | the bright fraction is nearly linear in [Fe/H] over the range of a population (25 to 45 percent between 0 and -1.5 at 500 Lsun), so the fraction at the mean stands for the mixture | a population whose spread is wide enough that the fraction at the mean differs from the mean of the fractions by more than the count noise | counts worked out per component at its own [Fe/H] and per disc radius | COMPUTED (the bright fraction of the isochrone tables at [Fe/H] 0 and -1.5, test_star_population) |
<!-- star-count-standins:end -->

### 6.50 Stand-ins of the stellar mass function (GEN.927, GEN.928)

Rows MF1 to MF3. The single-star mass function is Chabrier (2003), and the primaries' function is derived from it and the multiplicity rows, not set (`docs/design/galaxy/stellar-mass-function.md`). Tiers T1 to T9 and tweak levels L0 to L4 as in 6.6a (GEN.725); the lane's reading (Bug 1), for the owner to confirm.

### 6.51 Stand-ins of the stars after the main sequence (GEN.935)

A star's luminosity, temperature and radius after the main sequence are read from the MIST v1.2 tracks (`physics/post_main_sequence.py`, `docs/design/galaxy/post-main-sequence-stars.md`, tier T1 data), so none of the old hand-set ranges is left. What stays a choice is when each phase is entered and left, where the mass boundaries between the kinds of ending lie, and what the instability strip and Yerkes classes are called. Kinds, tiers and tweak levels as in 6.1.

### 6.52 Stand-ins of the stellar [X/Fe] refit to Brewer and Fischer (GEN.988, GEN.989, GEN.990, GEN.991)

`tuning.STELLAR_XFE_FITS`, `STELLAR_XFE_SIGMA` and `STELLAR_XFE_CORRELATION` were fitted to Bensby 2014 and Nissen 2014 (the means, with Brewer and Fischer 2016 only for the residual correlation of 844 dwarfs). The refit holds them to the full table of 1,615 dwarfs (`scripts/fit_stellar_xfe_refit.py`, bands in `src/tests/data/brewer_fischer_xfe_bands.json`, test `test_stellar_xfe_brewer_fischer.py`). Brewer and Fischer's abundances are differential to the Sun, so a solar-metallicity thin-disc star sits 0.06 dex under the Sun in Mg and 0.03 in Si and in O; the thin plateaus follow.

## 7. Gemini's first batch

### 7.1 Counts and recommendation

The paste of 10:52 AM EDT is about 34,000 characters. I count about 110 distinct factual claims (numbers, relations, attributions) and about 45 that can be checked against a paper reachable by us. I checked 20 against papers or computations already in hand: **8 agree, 7 are contradicted by a verified figure, 5 are unsupported, misattributed or internally inconsistent.** A full claim-by-claim check is its own job: I recommend one separate checking thread that works the other 25 checkable claims by opening the cited papers, and a second only if Boss wants the unverifiable theory content (the theory frontiers section, about 30 claims) screened for factual errors.

The paste is a copy of the document the thread received and sits unchanged at `/mnt/project-files/reports/gap-filling-plan-2026-10-17-files/gemini-batch-1-unverified.txt`.

### 7.2 The 20 claims

| # | Gemini, unverified | Check | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Halo star counts reach no lower than M* about 3.8e10 | the six measured haloes span 3.8e10 to 8e10 (Harmsen 2017, READ in GEN.406) | agrees |
| 2 | Ex situ fraction rises steeply with M*, about 0.1 at Milky Way mass | Rodriguez-Gomez 2016 abstract (READ): about 0.10 at that mass, rising | agrees |
| 3 | Scale height ratio thick/thin 2.5 to 3.8 | Yoachim and Dalcanton: 2.5 ± 30 percent (READ); ranges overlap | agrees |
| 4 | Scale length ratio 1.0 to 1.3 | Yoachim and Dalcanton formula gives 0.95 to 1.18 over 89 to 255 km/s | agrees |
| 5 | Vertical heating index 0.46 ± 0.03 | Sharma and Hayden 2020 Table 2: 0.441 ± 0.007 | agrees |
| 6 | Dispersions rise linearly with height; low- and high-alpha follow the same law; 3 to 20 kpc | Sharma and Hayden 2020 abstract, same wording as Gemini's sentence (Gemini cites "Sharma et al. 2021 GALAH DR3"; the paper read is Sharma and Hayden 2020) | agrees (attribution loose) |
| 7 | Mean cluster half-light radius 2.7 ± 0.3 pc, no dependence on cluster luminosity | Jordan 2005 abstract: 2.7 ± 0.35 pc, none on luminosity; but the paper finds dependence on host distance, surface brightness and colour, so "invariant across host luminosity" overstates | agrees (partly) |
| 8 | Blue clusters about 20 percent larger than red | Jordan 2005: red about 17 percent smaller, i.e. blue/red = 1.20 | agrees |
| 9 | Occupation half-point 10^8.6 Msun | Benitez-Llambay and Frenk 2020: 10^9.4 Msun/h for early reionization, only 4 times lower for z_re = 4, i.e. 10^8.8 at the extreme | contradicted |
| 10 | Occupation reaches 1 at 10^9.3 and 0 at 10^7.8 | the same paper: none below 3e8 (10^8.5), all above 5e9 (10^9.7) | contradicted |
| 11 | Thick/thin mass ratio log = 0.35 - 1.10 log(Vc/100) | gives 2.5, 1.2, 0.8 at 89, 181, 255 km/s, above the hard bound of 0.96, 0.64, 0.54 (4.4); also gives 0.94 at 220 km/s while the same passage says 0.2 to 0.45 above 200 | contradicted |
| 12 | sigma_z(10 Gyr) = 38.5 ± 1.8 km/s | Sharma and Hayden 2020 Table 2: 21.1 ± 0.2 | contradicted |
| 13 | sigma_R(10 Gyr) = 62.0 ± 2.5 km/s | same table: 39.4 ± 0.3 | contradicted |
| 14 | Radial heating index 0.42 ± 0.03 | same table: 0.251 ± 0.006 | contradicted |
| 15 | Nuclear cluster mass slope 0.65 ± 0.10, attributed to Neumayer 2020 | Neumayer 2020 gives 0.48; Georgiev gives 1.0 to 1.36 (both READ in GEN.406); 0.65 matches neither | contradicted |
| 16 | RMS of log(Vc/sigma0) = 0.12 dex, 64 galaxies | the Courteau 2007 text (read) prints no RMS; the passage also says both Vc = sqrt(2) sigma0 and Vc at least 2 sigma0 | unsupported, inconsistent |
| 17 | Blue/red cluster system radius ratio 1.20 ± 0.08 from Forbes 2012, Rhode and Zepf 2004, Kartha 2014 | the Kartha 2014 text searched, ratio not found; GEN.406 found papers that disagree (equal within errors against more extended) | unsupported |
| 18 | Low-mass stellar-halo relation slope 1.90, zero point 6.80, from UniverseMachine | Behroozi 2019 Appendix J fits only 10^10.5 to 10^15 (READ), so the paper has no low-mass parameterisation to cite | misattributed |
| 19 | Thick-disc start 12 to 13 Gyr lookback at z = 2.5 to 4.0 (Pinna 2019) | with Planck-like cosmology 12 Gyr is z = 3.5 and 13 Gyr is z = 6.8 (computed), and 2.5 to 4 is 11.2 to 12.3 Gyr; Pinna 2019 not located by search | inconsistent, unsupported |
| 20 | Halo formation redshift z50 = 1.20 - 0.35 log(M/1e12), from Aquarius, Cooper 2010, Deason 2016, D'Souza and Bell 2018 | Cooper 2010 haloes are Milky Way mass only; no relation of this form located in the sources we hold | unsupported |

Not counted: the occupation width 0.25 ± 0.04 dex (agrees with the 0.20 to 0.25 derived from the verified edges, 4.5) is in the same row as the contradicted centre and is kept in the bracket for GR15 as a LEAD that agrees.

### 7.2a Result of the checking thread (2026-10-17)

The checking thread finished at 11:49 AM EDT as it reported ([gemini-batch1-check.md](gemini-batch1-check.md)). It checked 51 claims (the 20 above plus 31 more): 10 agree, 17 partly, 16 contradicted by a read source, 4 unsupported, 4 misattributed. Five of the 20 above moved after more reading. The corrections that matter for the build items: the occupation centre is 10^9.33 Msun/h with width 0.21 dex (Gemini's 10^8.6 is the paper's no-reionization limit); the 1.9 low-mass stellar-to-halo slope is Garrison-Kimmel 2014 (1.92), not UniverseMachine; the halo formation redshift z50 is 1.02 at 1e10 and 0.74 at 1e12 (Gemini 1.9 and 1.2); the age-velocity normalisations are 21.1 and 39.4 km/s with indices 0.441 and 0.251 (Gemini 38.5, 62.0, 0.46, 0.42); the Courteau scatter is 0.097 dex about the paper's own line (79 markers digitised from its Fig 2; the 64 galaxies were a misreading). Requests now answered by a usable table: R2 (all three curves), R5, R12 (the z_re table with Busha's spread), R13 (Munoz ultra-faint sizes) and parts of R3, R6 and R10. Still open for Boss's list: R1, R4 beyond the Milky Way, R7, R8, R14 and parts of R6, R9, R10, R11. Three conflicts between two verified sources (thick-to-thin height ratio 2.5 Yoachim and Dalcanton against 4.2 Comerón 2018 median; thick-to-thin mass ratio against Vc; cluster system radius about 3.5 against about 6 times the galaxy's) are Decided, widen and keep both (Boss's cards, tapped 12:02 PM EDT (read from the session log and relayed in words by the coordinator)): the height ratio is drawn from 2.5 to 4.2, the mass ratio against Vc is widened between the two trends, the system radius ratio is drawn from about 3.5 to about 6, and every source stays in the record.

### 7.3 How results enter

Every Gemini figure is stored as a LEAD record next to the fill it concerns, with the claim number from 7.2, the verified figure it was checked against and a verdict. Agreeing claims raise the fill's evidence only if a verified source is also cited; contradicted claims are kept and show in the loader report as "LEAD rejected". The checking thread of 7.1 turns the remaining checkable claims into verdicts of the same shape; the second batch Boss expects uses the same intake. The theory frontier content (WDM, SIDM, fuzzy dark matter, thick-disc origin paradigms, assembly bias) is not a measurement and is never a default; it can only supply the form of a T-kind fill, for example the gas-rich turbulent collapse picture behind GR6, and it is labelled theory wherever it appears.

### 8.1 The papers Boss fetches (item 5)

Status, 3:07 PM EDT on 2026-10-18: Boss has already provided all the AD1 data he can; nothing on this list is waiting on him for AD1 (Karkoschka and Tomasko, Fray and Schmitt, Ayers 1980, ICRP 103). The rows below record what each paper would add if it is ever supplied.

The full list is [papers-to-fetch.md](papers-to-fetch.md): the nine papers Boss asked for first (section A there), the other papers the files could not read (B) and what is not needed (D), each with a Crossref citation and the places to try a free copy. Corrections to the first version of this table, from that list:

- BC3: Stixrude and Lithgow-Bertelloni has two parts, Part I (GJI 162, 610, 2005, doi 10.1111/j.1365-246x.2005.02642.x, bronze open access) and Part II (GJI 184, 1180, 2011, doi 10.1111/j.1365-246x.2010.04890.x); the 2024 paper III named earlier is what the second-hand copy carries.
- BC4: Palme and O'Neill and Rudnick and Gao each have a 2014 second edition of the Treatise on Geochemistry (prefer it) and an earlier first edition.
- BC7, BC12: Fray and Schmitt 2009 is unchanged; a HAL record exists. Bierson and Nimmo 2019 has a free author copy in PubMed Central (PMC7058130), which a thread can fetch itself.
- BC13: the free extract of ICRP 103 stops before the wR and wT tables (section 4, pages 61 to 78), so those pages are the ones to fetch.
- AD4: the numbers needed (depolarization of SO2 and H2S) are in Bogaard, Buckingham, Pierens and White 1978 (J. Chem. Soc., Faraday Trans. 1, 74, 3008, doi 10.1039/f19787403008), not in King 1923; King (Proc. R. Soc. A 104, 333, doi 10.1098/rspa.1923.0113) is the origin of the factor, so the item names both. Sneep and Ubachs 2005, Bates 1984 and Bucholtz 1995 (Appl. Opt. 34, 2765) stay on the list.
- AD7: Tabazadeh, Toon, Clegg and Hamill 1997 (GRL 24, 1931, bronze open access at Wiley) and Zeleznik 1991 (JPCRD 20, 1157).
- AD8: JPL Publication 19-5 is Burkholder et al. 2020, Evaluation No. 19.
- Free copies that make some "refused" rows readable by a thread: Lodders 2003 (IOP bronze PDF, for BC1), Sossi 2020 (open access, already read).
- On arXiv, so a research thread reads them and Boss need not: Aumer and Binney 2009 (0905.2512, GR4), Haywood 2013 (1305.4663) and Bensby 2014 (1309.2631) (GR6), Pinna 2019a and b (1901.04310, 1904.01260) (GR6), Graus 2019 (1808.03654, an inference) and Graus's FIRE age-gradient paper (1901.05487) (GR15). GR4, GR6 and GR15 therefore move from class B to class A in section 3.

## 9. Order of work

Grouped for builders; groups with no arrow between them run in parallel. "Gate" says what the group holds up.

| Group | Contents | Needs | Gate |
|---|---|---|---|
| X1 Registry | `fills.py` record, loader report, build check on "open" fills, docs tag; hooks into GEN.472 source states and the GEN.503 target table | nothing | first, because every other group registers into it |
| X2 Galaxy derived fills | GR3 bound, GR4 rule, GR12 and GR15, GR9a, GX1 | X1 | 8.1 phase 5 Galaxy laws build (GEN.401, 369, 403); GR4 feeds GEN.447, which GEN.301 names |
| X3 Accreted halo | integral of 4.2: GR1, GR10, GX5, GR14 with the slope test against 0.79 | X1, X2 (GX1 for halo mass given M*) | GEN.401, 403, 405, 386 |
| X4 Galaxy reading jobs | GR2 digitising, GR5, GR7, GR8, GR9b, GR9c, GR11, GR13 | none; research threads | GEN.402, 403, 369, and GR9c feeds GEN.280, which GEN.301 names |
| X5 Galaxy priors | GR6, GX2, GX3, GX4 | X3 (GR6 uses the GR10 epoch); Boss items 1, 2 and 7 Decided | GEN.386, 402, 403 |
| X6 Star and oxidation package | BC2 regression, BC1 water share, shared iron-oxidation variable, AT.a1 and a2, BC4 major oxides, BC15, BC16 | X1 | 9.0; building blocks already back-scattered to 8.1 (GEN.472 phase 2) |
| X7 Body reading jobs | BC3, BC4 originals, BC7, BC9, BC10, BC12, BC13, BC19 | the originals wait for the files Boss fetches (item 5, 8.1); BC19 downloads at build time (6.5) | 9.0 |
| X8 Theory priors | BC5, BC6, BC8, BC14, BC17, BC18, AT.b4, AT.k7, AT.j, AT.m | X6 for the shared variables | 9.0 |
| X9 Atmosphere data | AD1 to AD8 (downloads and extractions) | the download and offline-test design (6.5), not the licence read; AD4, AD7 and AD8 originals wait for Boss's files | 9.0 |
| X10 Placeholders | BC11, AT.b5, AT.n7 | none (Boss item 3 Decided) | 9.0 |
| X11 Thin F rows | the 16 AT.F rows | the tunable-gaps builders | 9.0 |

Which gaps block what, as far as I traced:

- **Block the 8.1 headline chain**: the two gaps named in GEN.301's own prerequisite line, GR4 (through GEN.447) and GR9c (through GEN.280). GR4 is derived and ready (X2). GR9c is a bracket with a virial-theorem part and one reading job (X4); it can ship as a labelled bracket without waiting for the review. I did not trace the other prerequisites of GEN.301 to gaps, so there may be more.
- **Block the 8.1 stamp** (the six-phase "Galaxy laws build" items listed in the lane report): all galaxy rows of 5.1, in the order X2, X3, X4, X5. Boss decided items 1, 2, 6 and 7 of section 8, and each has a fill that ships without further answers (item 6 also runs the quick VizieR search first).
- **Do not block 8.1**: every body and atmosphere row. They belong to 9.0, with the generation changes that alter stored values staying with 8.4 as already Decided; X6 is the exception worth pulling early because its outputs (the star regression and the water-share function) are inputs to GEN.472 phase 2 and the 8.1 back-scattered building blocks.
- **Epoch**: a fill that replaces a value later changes stored output and rides the pending epoch; the X1 registry records the epoch per fill so the builder of GEN.322-324 style rescatters can see which stand-ins sat inside it.

## 10. Limits of this report

- Nothing in the repository was changed. No papers were taken from refused publishers. Every number marked COMPUTED can be reproduced with the scripts in the files folder.
- READ means a thread opened the paper or table; abstracts only are marked "abstract READ". The Jordan 2005 and Rodriguez-Gomez 2016 statements rest on abstracts. Nothing in 7.2 is more than one check deep.
- The accreted-halo integral is a sketch: it applies the Moster z = 0 relation at the accretion mass, ignores satellite stripping, and over-predicts by a factor of 2 to 3 against Illustris. It is a shape source and a test, not a law.
- The oxygen-budget water share has no disk chemistry. The age-velocity rule has one closure target (the Milky Way) and so cannot yet be tested on a second galaxy.
- The Brewer 2016 regressions use the whole catalogue without its quality cut; the cut must be applied by the builder (4.7).
- Gemini claim counts (110 distinct, 45 checkable) are my estimates from reading the paste; the 20 checked are exactly those in 7.2.

Files: `/mnt/project-files/reports/gap-filling-plan-2026-10-17-files/` holds `water_share.py`, `acc.py`, `ws_stars.py`, `arx.py`, `brewer2016_vizier_J_ApJS_225_32.tsv` and `gemini-batch-1-unverified.txt`.

As built (GEN.498): `physics/oxygen_fugacity.py` fixes the one oxygen-fugacity scale, the Hirschmann 2021 iron-wustite buffer, and every stored delta-IW is on it (`mantle_delta_iw` was already read that way: Earth's upper mantle at IW+3.5 is QFM, which stands 3.1 to 3.7 above IW). Checked against the papers Boss supplied (9:55 PM to 10:17 PM EDT on 2026-10-22, from the coordinator's wake times): Hirschmann's Table 1 (every coefficient, sign and the iron-phase boundary), the NASA Glenn coefficients of CO, CO2, H2, H2O and O2, and Sossi et al. 2020 equation 4 are verified; the stand-ins that remain, each a row of `oxygen_fugacity.SOURCES`: the Frost 1991 FMQ and NNO constants (the text is paywalled; the form is confirmed by Anenburg 2026 and their gap at 1 GPa agrees with it, the absolute values are second hand from PetroBuffer), and the Sossi scale's 0.5 offset (derived from his gas ratios; his IW formula is in his Supplementary Materials, not read; Hirschmann's JANAF offset explains its size). Nothing stored changes.

As built (GEN.470, row BC12): `physics/saturation.py` and `physics/data/saturation_pressure.json` hold the one vapour-pressure function `saturation(species, T)`, in Pa with a status (`ok`, `cold-unverified` below 50 K, `below-fit` under the polynomial's own turning point, `above-triple`). It carries the Fray and Schmitt 2009 polynomials for Ne, N2, CO, Ar, O2, CH4, Kr, Xe, C2H6, C2H4, C2H2, H2S, CO2, NH3, SO2, HCN, CH3OH and C6H6 as quoted in the supplementary table of Lisse et al. (2ND-HAND; O2-3 and Ne-2 from part 01 section 7.2) and water by Feistel and Wagner (76.03 Pa at 250 K, 0.1626 Pa at 200 K, 611.657 Pa at the triple point). **Stand-ins, because the paper's per-fit windows are not read (BC12 stays open):** where a species has more than one fit the next takes over where the two cross (N2 36.1 K, CO 60.75 K, O2 23.15 and 43.79 K, CH3OH 157.55 K, Ne 13.26 K; each within 1 K of the known solid transition) or come closest (CO2 195.3 K, 1.1 percent apart); every `floor_k` is derived from the polynomial. CO2-1 serves the cold, not the CO2-2 that part 01 recommends from its triple-point error alone, because CO2-2 reaches 1e-9 bar at 64.7 K against the 86 K the latent heat of sublimation gives; H2S-1 alone, as part 01 does. **Known gaps:** at the triple point SO2 is 3.8 percent over the gas table's pressure, benzene 7 percent over and acetylene 3.6 percent under, every other species within 3 percent; H2 and He have no curve; above the triple point the liquid branch of the next paragraph takes over. GEN.497 replaces the cold end and the solid CO2 curve; GEN.452 makes the atmosphere read the function.

As built (GEN.497, row BC12, the cold end): `saturation` now serves five species from the laboratory fits `ln P[bar] = A - B/T + C ln T + D2 T + D3 T^2 + D4 T^3` (Lobo and Ferreira 2001) of Grundy et al. 2024 (N2, CO, CH4) and Blakley et al. 2024 (O2, NH3) instead of the Fray and Schmitt polynomial in the cold, and carbon dioxide from Span and Wagner 1996 equation 3.12 for the whole solid (842.65 Pa at 150 K, 103971 Pa at 195 K, 101325 Pa at 194.6855 K; the two Fray and Schmitt CO2 fits are gone). Each laboratory fit is used from the bottom of its fit range (N2 20 K, CO 25, CH4 30, O2 30, NH3 80) up to where it meets the polynomial (N2 35 K, the top of its range, with a published step to the alpha polynomial of a factor of 2.1; CO 54.4 K; CH4 51.0; O2 42.7; NH3 162.3); inside it the status is `ok`, under it the curve is Clausius-Clapeyron with the latent heat the fit has at its edge, flagged `below-fit`, and above it the polynomial keeps `cold-unverified` below 50 K. `tuning.COLD_END_LABORATORY_CORRECTION` (on) is the switch for the 2026 Grundy preprint, which multiplies all five by 1.19 +/- 0.03: on is its Table 3 A and B with the 2024 constants C to D4 (N2, CO and CH4: the reconstructions of part G4, max 0.6 percent), off is the 2024 set (N2, CO and CH4: the same curve divided by 1.19). Both sets and their hand-over temperatures are in the data file. **Stand-ins, labelled:** the fit range of each curve is the range of Grundy's data package and Blakley's data (finding 21 of the atmosphere composition research lists the narrower measured windows, N2 23 to 31 K, CO 26 to 35, CH4 31 to 43, O2 29 to 37, NH3 88 to 105); the Clausius-Clapeyron continuation below the range has no width stated (the "doubled in width" of the BC12 decision is not carried yet); the N2 step; the C to D4 of N2, CO and CH4 are reconstructions. BC12 (the original per-fit windows) stays open.

As built (GEN.452, the liquid branch of `saturation`): above the triple point and up to the critical point `saturation` returns the Wagner fits to the NIST saturation tables of part 01 section 6.2 (COMPUTED by the Atmosphere research lane, within 0.26 percent of the table), hydrogen cyanide the Perry DIPPR 101 correlation, and water the Wagner fit (3538.6 Pa at 300 K against the steam table's 3536.8). Above the critical temperature the status is `above-critical` and the pressure is infinite; acetylene has no liquid curve. Hydrogen is a Clausius-Clapeyron solid from its triple point with 1.02 kJ/mol (0.90 vapourisation from the gas table plus 0.12 fusion, RECALLED, a stand-in unchecked against Woolley 1948). The solid and liquid curves meet within the part-01 spread (N2 -2.7 percent, CH4 and H2S +1.7, NH3 +1.9, HCN +3.3, SO2 +3.8, benzene +7.1, others within 1 percent), so a step at the triple point is documented, not smoothed.

As built (GEN.452, the swap): `physics/atmosphere.py` no longer holds a vapour-pressure constant. `CONDENSATION_K` and the `h2s` copy line are gone and `vapour_pressure_kpa` reads `saturation.pressure_pa` (infinite above the critical temperature, so a hot world caps nothing), and `saturation.condensation_temperature_k(species, pressure)` gives the inverse (bisection, `None` above the critical pressure). The twelve other rows of the checked table of part 01 section 7.4 (H2, N2, CO, Ar, O2, CH4, SO2, H2O, H2S, NH3, C2H6, HCN at seven pressures from 100 kPa to 1 mPa) were reproduced to 0.12 K by the curves as they stood when the table was made; after the laboratory cold ends of GEN.497 and the Span and Wagner carbon dioxide curve the test allows 1 K above 60 K and 2 K below. **The thirteenth, CO2, departs from that table on purpose:** the table used CO2-2, which is 4 K cold at 100 Pa and 17 K cold at 1 mPa; the carbon dioxide curve is now the Span and Wagner sublimation equation (GEN.497). The cold gases (N2, CO, CH4) follow the laboratory cold ends, up to about 1.6 K from the table below 60 K; the test tolerates that. N2 at 37 K is 1.2 Pa (Pluto's air, about 1 Pa), where the old table gave 10 Pa. The rogue-planet nitrogen frost (`rogue_surface.nitrogen_vapor_pressure_pa`) and the water curve of `hydrosphere.py` still hold their own constants; moving them is a stored-value change for GEN.461 and the rogue-surface item.
